Continuing work from thread T-019b3688-6a6f-70cf-9978-6f6214b6366d. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md @src/editable/mod.rs @src/editable/state.rs @src/editable/messages.rs @src/editable/context.rs @src/update/text_edit.rs @src/update/mod.rs @src/messages.rs @src/update/editor.rs @src/model/document.rs

Other relevant files: src/update/document.rs, src/model/editor.rs, docs/CHANGELOG.md

I am implementing a unified text editing system for the editor. The comprehensive plan is at docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md.

Completed phases:
- Phase 1: Core data model (src/editable/ module with EditableState, StringBuffer, Cursor, Selection, constraints)
- Phase 2: Message system (Msg::TextEdit variant, src/update/text_edit.rs handler)
- Phase 3: TextFieldRenderer for unified rendering (src/view/text_field.rs)
- Phase 4a-c: Modal states (CommandPaletteState, GotoLineState, FindReplaceState) updated to use EditableState<StringBuffer>
- Phase 4d: Wired update_text_edit() to route TextEditMsg to modal EditableStates
- Phase 4e: Updated modal rendering to use TextFieldRenderer
- Phase 4f: Added full keyboard navigation for modals (Left/Right, Home/End, Shift+Arrow for selection, Option+Arrow for word movement, Cmd+A for select all, Delete key)
- Milestone 3: CSV Cell Migration - CellEditState now uses EditableState<StringBuffer> with word movement, word deletion, select all, and undo/redo support

Key patterns I'm following:
- Using from_text() for buffer constructors
- Modal states expose input(), set_input(), cursor() methods
- EditableState provides move_left(), move_right(), move_line_start(), move_line_end(), move_word_left(), move_word_right(), select_all(), delete_forward(), delete_backward(), delete_word_backward(), delete_word_forward(), undo(), redo() etc.
- TextFieldRenderer::render() takes &dyn TextFieldContent and TextFieldOptions
- CellEditState wraps EditableState<StringBuffer> and exposes convenience methods like buffer(), insert_char(), cursor_left(), cursor_word_left(), etc.

Next phases according to the plan:
- Milestone 6: Main Editor Bridge - Create a bridge that maps TextEditMsg to existing EditorMsg/DocumentMsg so the unified message system can control the main editor
- Milestone 8: Cleanup and documentation - Remove deprecated code, finalize docs

The bridge approach (Milestone 6) is described in the plan around line 3025-3055. It maps TextEditMsg variants to existing messages like:
- TextEditMsg::Move(MoveTarget::Left) -> EditorMsg::MoveCursor(Direction::Left)
- TextEditMsg::InsertChar(ch) -> DocumentMsg::InsertChar(ch)
- TextEditMsg::DeleteBackward -> DocumentMsg::DeleteBackward

Remaining TODO items from test checklists:
- CSV: Selection (Shift+Arrow), Cut/Copy/Paste integration
- Modals: Clipboard integration

Version is now 0.3.8. All tests pass.

lets continue with the next phases

StringBuffer.\n99: /// Returns true if the state was modified (needs redraw).\n100: pub fn apply_text_edit_msg(\n101:     state: &mut EditableState<StringBuffer>,\n102:     msg: &TextEditMsg,\n103: ) -> bool {\n104:     match msg {\n105:         // === Movement ===\n106:         TextEditMsg::Move(target) => {\n107:             apply_move(state, *target, false);\n108:             true\n109:         }\n110:         TextEditMsg::MoveWithSelection(target) => {\n111:             apply_move(state, *target, true);\n112:             true\n113:         }\n114: \n115:         // === Insertion ===\n116:   }\n132: \n133:         // === Selection ===\n134:         TextEditMsg::SelectAll => {\n135:             state.select_all();\n136:             true\n137:         }\n138: single-line, select_all is equivalent\n144:             state.select_all();\n145:             true\n146:         }\n147:         TextEditMsg::CollapseSelection => {\n148:             state.collapse_selection();\n149:             true\n150:         }\n151: \n152:         // === Multi-Cursor (not supported in single-line contexts) ===\n153:         TextEditMsg::AddCursorAbove\n154: false,\n158: \n159:         // === Clipboard ===\n160:         TextEditMsg::Copy => {\n161:             // Caller should handle clipboard access\n162:             // We just indicate whether there's something to copy\n163:             state.has_selection()\n164:         }\n165:         TextEditMsg::Cut => {\n166:             if state.has_selection() {\n167:                 state.delete_backward();\n168:                 true\n169:             } else {\n170:                 false\n171:             }\n172:         }\n173:         TextEditMsg::Paste(text) => state.insert_text(text),\n174: \n175:         // === Undo/Redo ===\n176:         TextEditMsg::Undo => state.undo(),\n177:         TextEditMsg::Redo => state.redo(),\n178: \n179:         // === Line TextBufferMut>(\n190:     state: &mut EditableState<B>,\n191:     target: MoveTarget,\n192:     extend_selection: bool,\n193: ) {\n194:     match target {\n195:         MoveTarget::Left => state.move_left(extend_selection),\n196:         MoveTarget::Right => state.move_right(extend_selection),\n197:         MoveTarget::Up => state.move_up(extend_selection),\n198:   "content": "1: //! Message types for the Elm-style architecture\n2: //!\n3: //! All state changes flow through these message types.\n4: \n5: use std::path::PathBuf;\n6: \n7: use crate::editable::{EditContext, TextEditMsg};\n8: \n9: /// Direction for cursor movement\n10: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n11: pub enum Direction {\n12:     Up,\n13:     Down,\n14:     Left,\n15:     Right,\n16: }\n17: \n18: /// Editor-specific messages (cursor movement, viewport scrolling)\n19: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n20: pub enum EditorMsg {\n21:     // === Basic Movement ===\n22:     /// Move cursor in a direction\n23:     MoveCursor(Direction),\n24:     /// Move cursor to start of line (Home key)\n25:     MoveCursorLineStart,\n26:     /// Move cursor to end of line (End key)\n27:     MoveCursorLineEnd,\n28:     /// Move cursor to start of document (Ctrl+Home)\n29:     MoveCursorDocumentStart,\n30:     /// Move cursor to end of document (Ctrl+End)\n31:     MoveCursorDocumentEnd,\n32:     /// Move cursor by word (Option+Left/Right on Mac)\n33:     MoveCursorWord(Direction),\n34:     /// Page up\n35:     PageUp,\n36:     /// Page down\n37:     PageDown,\n38:     /// Set cursor to specific position (from mouse click)\n39:     SetCursorPosition { line: usize, column: usize },\n40:     /// Scroll viewport vertically (positive = down, negative = up)\n41:     Scroll(i32),\n42:     /// Scroll viewport horizontally (positive = right, negative = left)\n43:     ScrollHorizontal(i32),\n44: \n45:     // === Selection Movement (Shift+key) ===\n46:     /// Move cursor with selection (Shift+Arrow)\n47:     MoveCursorWithSelection(Direction),\n48:     /// Move to line start with selection (Shift+Home)\n49:     MoveCursorLineStartWithSelection,\n50:     /// Move to line end with selection (Shift+End)\n51:     MoveCursorLineEndWithSelection,\n52:     /// Move to document start with selection (Shift+Ctrl+Home)\n53:     MoveCursorDocumentStartWithSelection,\n54:     /// Move to document end with selection (Shift+Ctrl+End)\n55:     MoveCursorDocumentEndWithSelection,\n56:     /// Move word with selection (Shift+Option+Arrow)\n57:     MoveCursorWordWithSelection(Direction),\n58:     /// Page up with selection\n59:     PageUpWithSelection,\n60:     /// Page down with selection\n61:     PageDownWithSelection,\n62: \n63:     // === Selection Commands ===\n64:     /// Select all text (Cmd+A)\n65:     SelectAll,\n66:     /// Select word at cursor (double-click)\n67:     SelectWord,\n68:     /// Select entire line (triple-click)\n69:     SelectLine,\n70:     /// Extend selection to position (Shift+Click)\n71:     ExtendSelectionToPosition { line: usize, column: usize },\n72:     /// Clear all selections (collapse to cursors)\n73:     ClearSelection,\n74: \n75:     // === Multi-Cursor ===\n76:     /// Toggle cursor at position (Option+Click)\n77:     ToggleCursorAtPosition { line: usize, column: usize },\n78:     /// Add cursor above current (Option+Option+Up)\n79:     AddCursorAbove,\n80:     /// Add cursor below current (Option+Option+Down)\n81:     AddCursorBelow,\n82:     /// Collapse to single cursor (Escape with multiple cursors)\n83:     CollapseToSingleCursor,\n84:     /// Remove cursor by Messages are mapped to their\n+/// legacy equivalents and dispatched through existing handlers.\n+fn bridge_text_edit_to_editor(model: &mut AppModel, msg: TextEditMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {\n+    match msg {\n+        // === Movement (no selection) ===\n+        TextEditMsg::Move(target) => {\n+            let editor_msg = match target {\n+                MoveTarget::Left => EditorMsg::MoveCursor(Direction::Left),\n+                MoveTarget::Right => EditorMsg::MoveCursor(Direction::Right),\n+                MoveTarget::Up => EditorMsg::MoveCursor(Direction::Up),\n+                MoveTarget::Down => EditorMsg::MoveCursor(Direction::Down),\n+                MoveTarget::LineStart => EditorMsg::MoveCursorLineStart,\n+                MoveTarget::LineEnd => EditorMsg::MoveCursorDocumentStart,\n+                MoveTarget::DocumentEnd => EditorMsg::MoveCursorDocumentEnd,\n+                MoveTarget::PageUp => EditorMsg::PageUp,\n+                MoveTarget::PageDown => EditorMsg::PageDown,\n+            };\n+            update_editor(model, editor_msg)\n+        }\n+\n+        // === Movement with selection ===\n+        TextEditMsg::MoveWithSelection(target) => {\n+            let editor_msg = match target {\n+                MoveTarget::Left => EditorMsg::MoveCursorWithSelection(Direction::Left),\n+                MoveTarget::Right => EditorMsg::MoveCursorWithSelection(Direction::Right),\n+                MoveTarget::Up => EditorMsg::MoveCursorWithSelection(Direction::Up),\n+                MoveTarget::Down => EditorMsg::MoveCursorWithSelection(Direction::Down),\n+                MoveTarget::LineStart => EditorMsg::MoveCursorLineStartWithSelection,\n+                MoveTarget::LineEnd => EditorMsg::MoveCursorLineEndWithSelection,\n+                MoveTarget::LineStartSmart => EditorMsg::MoveCursorLineStartWithSelection,\n+                MoveTarget::WordLeft => EditorMsg::MoveCursorWordWithSelection(Direction::Left),\n+                MoveTarget::WordRight => EditorMsg::MoveCursorWordWithSelection(Direction::Right),\n+                MoveTarget::DocumentStart => EditorMsg::MoveCursorDocumentStartWithSelection,\n+                MoveTarget::DocumentEnd => EditorMsg::MoveCursorDocumentEndWithSelection,\n+                MoveTarget::PageUp => EditorMsg::PageUpWithSelection,\n+                MoveTarget::PageDown => EditorMsg::PageDownWithSelection,\n+            };\n+            update_editor(model, editor_msg)\n+        }\n+\n+        // === Insertion ===\n+        TextEditMsg::InsertChar(ch) => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::InsertChar(ch)),\n+        TextEditMsg::InsertText(ref text) => {\n+            // Insert text character by character (no batch insert in legacy)\n+            let mut cmd = None;\n+            for ch in text.chars() {\n+                cmd = update_document(model, DocumentMsg::InsertChar(ch));\n+            }\n+            cmd\n+        }\n+        TextEditMsg::InsertNewline => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::InsertNewline),\n+\n+        // === Deletion ===\n+        TextEditMsg::DeleteBackward => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::DeleteBackward),\n+        TextEditMsg::DeleteForward => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::DeleteForward),\n+        TextEditMsg::DeleteWordBackward => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::DeleteWordBackward),\n+        TextEditMsg::DeleteWordForward => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::DeleteWordForward),\n+        TextEditMsg::DeleteLine => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::DeleteLine),\n+\n+        // === Selection ===\n+        TextEditMsg::SelectAll => update_editor(model, EditorMsg::SelectAll),\n+        TextEditMsg::SelectWord => update_editor(model, EditorMsg::SelectWord),\n+        TextEditMsg::SelectLine => update_editor(model, EditorMsg::SelectLine),\n+        TextEditMsg::CollapseSelection => update_editor(model, EditorMsg::ClearSelection),\n+\n+        // === Multi-Cursor ===\n+        TextEditMsg::AddCursorAbove => update_editor(model, EditorMsg::AddCursorAbove),\n+        TextEditMsg::AddCursorBelow => update_editor(model, EditorMsg::AddCursorBelow),\n+        TextEditMsg::AddCursorAtNextOccurrence => {\n+            update_editor(model, EditorMsg::SelectNextOccurrence)\n+        }\n+        TextEditMsg::AddCursorsAtAllOccurrences => {\n+            update_editor(model, EditorMsg::SelectAllOccurrences)\n+        }\n+        TextEditMsg::CollapseCursors => update_editor(model, EditorMsg::CollapseToSingleCursor),\n+\n+        // === Clipboard ===\n+        TextEditMsg::Copy => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::Copy),\n+        TextEditMsg::Cut => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::Cut),\n+        TextEditMsg::Paste(_) => {\n+            // The bridge approach uses the legacy Paste which reads from clipboard\n+            update_document(model, DocumentMsg::Paste)\n+        }\n+\n+        // === Undo/Redo ===\n+        TextEditMsg::Undo => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::Undo),\n+        TextEditMsg::Redo => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::Redo),\n+\n+        // === Line Operations ===\n+        TextEditMsg::Indent => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::IndentLines),\n+        TextEditMsg::Unindent => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::UnindentLines),\n+        TextEditMsg::Duplicate => update_document(model, DocumentMsg::Duplicate),\n+        TextEditMsg::MoveLineUp | TextEditMsg::MoveLineDown => **`TextBuffer` / `TextBufferMut` traits** - Abstract over String and Rope buffer backends\n15: - **`StringBuffer`** - Efficient single-line buffer for modals and CSV cells\n16: - **`EditableState<B>`** - Unified state container with cursor, selection, and undo history\n17: - **`EditConstraints`** - Context-specific restrictions (multiline, multi-cursor, char filters)\n18: - **`TextEditMsg` / `MoveTarget`** - Unified message types for all editing operations\n19: - **`EditContext`** - Identifies which input area is being edited\n20: - **`TextFieldRenderer`** - Unified text field rendering with selection support\n21: \n22: **Modal Input Improvements:**\n23: - Full cursor navigation in all modals (Left/Right, Home/End)\n24: - Selection support (Shift+Arrow) in command palette, goto line, find/replace\n25: - Word movement (Option+Arrow) in all modals\n26: - Word deletion (Option+Backspace/Delete) in all modals\n27: - Select all (Cmd+A) in all modals\n28: - Undo/redo within modal inputs\n29: - Delete forward (Delete key) now works in modals\n30: \n31: **CSV Cell Editor Enhancements:**\n32: - Migrated `CellEditState` to use `EditableState<StringBuffer>`\n33: - Word movement (Option+Left/Right) while editing cells\n34: - Word deletion (Option+Backspace/Delete) while editing cells\n35: - Select all (Cmd+A) while editing cells\n36: - Undo/redo (Cmd+Z / Cmd+Shift+Z) within cell editing session\n37: \n38: **New Messages:**\n39: - `CsvMsg::EditCursorWordLeft`, `EditCursorWordRight`\n40: - `CsvMsg::EditDeleteWordBackward`, `EditDeleteWordForward`\n41: - Click-and-drag to resize sidebar width\n67: - ColResize cursor shown on hover over resize border\n68: - `SidebarResizeState` tracks drag operation\n69: - Width persists in logical pixels for DPI-independence\n70: \n71: **File Tree Keyboard Navigation:**\n72: - ## Assistant

All done. Here's a summary of what was completed:

### Completed: Milestone 6 & 8

#### Milestone 6: Main Editor Bridge

Implemented `bridge_text_edit_to_editor()` in [src/update/text_edit.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/text_edit.rs#L218-L318) that maps `TextEditMsg` variants to legacy `EditorMsg`/`DocumentMsg`:

- **Movement**: `MoveTarget::Left/Right/Up/Down` → `EditorMsg::MoveCursor(Direction::*)`
- **Selection movement**: `MoveWithSelection(*)` → `EditorMsg::MoveCursor*WithSelection`
- **Word/Line/Document movement**: All mapped correctly
- **Editing**: `InsertChar`, `InsertText`, `InsertNewline`, `Delete*` → `DocumentMsg::*`
- **Selection commands**: `SelectAll/Word/Line` → `EditorMsg::*`
- **Multi-cursor**: `AddCursor*`, `CollapseCursors` → `EditorMsg::*`
- **Clipboard**: `Copy/Cut/Paste` → `DocumentMsg::*`
- **Undo/Redo**: `Undo/Redo` → `DocumentMsg::*`
- **Line operations**: `Indent/Unindent/Duplicate` → `DocumentMsg::*`

The bridge allows the unified `Msg::TextEdit(EditContext::Editor, msg)` message to control the main editor while maintaining full backwards compatibility.

#### Milestone 8: Documentation

- Updated [docs/CHANGELOG.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/CHANGELOG.md#L44-L48) with Main Editor Bridge section
- Updated [docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md#L10-L24) progress table - all milestones now marked complete

All 259 tests pass, lint is clean.

  "content": "1: //! Keyboard input handling\n2: //!\n3: //! This module handles keyboard input that requires special imperative logic\n4: //! beyond what the declarative keymap system can express.\n5: //!\n6: //! Most keybindings are handled by the keymap system in `src/keymap/`.\n7: //! This file handles:\n8: //! - Modal input routing (when a modal dialog is active)\n9: //! - CSV cell editing input routing\n10: //! - Option double-tap for multi-cursor creation\n11: //! - Navigation with selection collapse (moving clears selection first)\n12: //! - Character input (regular typing)\n13: \n14: use winit::keyboard::{Key, NamedKey};\n15: \n16: use token::commands::Cmd;\n17: use token::messages::{\n18:     CsvMsg, Direction, DocumentMsg, EditorMsg, LayoutMsg, ModalMsg, Msg, UiMsg, WorkspaceMsg,\n19: };\n20: use token::model::AppModel;\n21: use token::update::update;\n22: All done! Here's a summary of what was implemented:

### Summary

#### CSV Cell Editing Enhancements
- **Selection support** (Shift+Arrow, Shift+Home/End, Shift+Option+Arrow)
- **Clipboard integration** (Cmd+C to copy, Cmd+X to cut, Cmd+V to paste)
- New `CsvMsg` variants: `EditCursorLeftWithSelection`, `EditCursorRightWithSelection`, `EditCursorHomeWithSelection`, `EditCursorEndWithSelection`, `EditCursorWordLeftWithSelection`, `EditCursorWordRightWithSelection`, `EditCopy`, `EditCut`, `EditPaste`

#### Modal Input Enhancements
- **Clipboard integration** (Cmd+C to copy, Cmd+X to cut, Cmd+V to paste)
- New `ModalMsg` variants: `Copy`, `Cut`, `Paste`
- Paste filters out newlines for single-line inputs
- Paste in Go-to-Line filters to only digits

#### Files Modified
- [src/messages.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/messages.rs) - Added new message variants
- [src/csv/model.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/csv/model.rs) - Added selection movement and insert_text methods
- [src/update/csv.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/csv.rs) - Added handlers for selection and clipboard
- [src/update/ui.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/ui.rs) - Added modal clipboard handlers
- [src/runtime/input.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/input.rs) - Wired up keyboard shortcuts
- [docs/CHANGELOG.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/CHANGELOG.md) - Updated with new features
- [docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/feature/unified-text-editing-final.md) - Marked checklists complete

Building from source\n17: \n18: To build Token from source, you will need the [Rust toolchain](https://rustup.rs/) installed.\n19: The `makefile setup` takes care of installing any other dependencies.\n20: \n21: ```shell\n22: git clone https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token\n23: cd token\n24: \n25: # Install dependencies (and misc tools)\n26: make setup\n27: \n28: # Build and run with Makefile\n29: make dev\n30: \n31: # Or run seperately\n32: make build\n33: make run\n34: \n35: # Or build and run manually with cargo,\n36: # like a regular boring person...\n37: cargo build --release\n38: cargo run\n39: ```\n40: \n41: ## Commands\n42: \n43: List them with `make help`\n44: \n45: ### Build & Run\n46: \n47: | Command        | Description                                      |\n48: | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |\n49: | `make build`   | Build debug binary                               |\n50: | `make release` | Build optimized release binary                   |\n51: | `make run`     | Run with default sample file (indentation.txt)   |\n52: | `make dev`     | Run debug build (faster compile, slower runtime) |\n53: | `make csv`     | Run with large CSV file (tests CSV viewer)       |\n54: | `make clean`   | Remove build artifacts                           |\n55: | `make fmt`     | Format Rust code and markdown files              |\n56: \n57: ### Testing\n58: \n59: | Command             | Description                            |\n60: | ------------------- | -------------------------------------- |\n61: | `make test`         | Run all tests                          |\n62: | `make test-verbose` | Run tests with output                  |\n63: | `make test-fast`    | Run tests faster using `cargo nextest` |\n64: \n65: ### Features\n66: \n67: | Feature                  | Description                                                                  |\n68: | ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n69: | Multi-cursor editing     | Option+Click to add cursors, Option+Option+Arrow to add above/below          |\n70: | Split views              | Horizontal and vertical splits with independent viewports                    |\n71: | Syntax highlighting      | Tree-sitter based, 17 languages supported                                    |\n72: | **CSV Viewer**           | Spreadsheet view for CSV/TSV/PSV files (Command Palette → \"Toggle CSV View\") |\n73: | Configurable keybindings | YAML-based keymap with context-aware bindings                                |\n74: | Themes                   | Dark and light themes with full customization                                |\n75: \n76: ---\n77: \n78: ## The Hidden Complexity of Text Editors\n79: \n80: You use a text editor every day. But do you actually understand what it's doing?\n81: \n82: On the surface, an editor is \"just\" text on a screen with a blinking cursor. Underneath, it's a small physics engine for\n83: glyphs, pixels, and input events—quietly solving dozens of problems every time you press a key.\n84: \n85: Consider a few things you rely on constantly but probably don't think about:\n86: \n87: - **Cursor + selection choreography** — Hold Shift and press arrow keys: the cursor moves, the selection grows or\n88:   shrinks, and the viewport sometimes nudges just enough to keep everything visible. Double-click a word, then\n89:   Shift+Click somewhere else. The rules feel obvious—until you try to implement them.\n90: \n91: - **Keyboard modifier edge cases** — What's the \"right\" behavior for Ctrl+Left on a line with emoji, snake_case, and\n92:   camelCase? What happens to your selection when you hit Cmd+Z, then Cmd+Shift+Z, then type? When should Alt extend\n93:   selection, Cmd jump by word, or Option+Option+Arrow add a new cursor?\n94: \n95: - **Glyphs that don't behave like characters** — A single \"character\" on screen might be multiple codepoints: combining\n96:   accents, zero-width joiners, ligatures. The cursor should snap to visually sensible positions, not halfway through a\n97:   grapheme cluster. Column count, byte offset, and rendered width all disagree—and the editor must reconcile them.\n98: \n99: - **Viewport scrolling that feels \"natural\"** — Page Down doesn't simply add `viewportHeight` to `scrollY`. It decides\n100:   which line becomes the new top, keeps the cursor in a \"safe zone,\" and handles partial lines at edges. Mouse wheel,\n101:   scrollbar drag, and \"Go to Line\" all produce subtly different scroll behavior—but never feel inconsistent when done\n102:   right.\n103: \n104: All of this hides behind a UI that's supposed to feel invisible. That's exactly why it's easy to underestimate the\n105: complexity.\n106: \n107: **Why this matters for AI-assisted development:** If you want AI agents to help design, debug, or extend an editor, they\n108: need more than \"there's a cursor and some lines.\" They need a precise, shared vocabulary for scroll offsets, viewports,\n109: cursor modes, selection semantics, and edge cases. The same is true for humans: explicit documentation is how you\n110: sanity-check your own assumptions and avoid rediscovering tricky behaviors through trial and error.\n111: \n112: Token is built around that idea: make geometry, behavior, and edge cases **fully explicit**—for both humans and\n113: machines. See [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) for our attempt to turn \"obvious\" editor behavior\n114: into a concrete, testable spec.\n115: \n116: ---\n117: \n118: ## AI-Assisted Development: A Framework for Complex Projects\n119: \n120: Token was built primarily through conversations with [Amp Code](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre). This section\n121: documents the methodology that made it work—applicable to any non-trivial project where you're collaborating with AI\n122: agents.\n123: \n124: ### The Core Principle\n125: \n126: AI agents excel at focused, well-defined tasks. The human's job is to provide **structure**: clear phases, written\n127: specifications, and explicit invariants. The more complex your project, the more this structure pays off.\n128: \n129: ---\n130: \n131: ### Three Modes of Work\n132: \n133: Before each session, explicitly state which mode you're in:\n134: \n135: | Mode        | Purpose                                       | Inputs                       | Example                          |\n136: | ----------- | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------- |\n137: | **Build**   | New behavior that didn't exist                | Feature spec, reference docs | \"Implement split view (Phase 3)\" |\n138: | **Improve** | Better architecture without changing behavior | Organization docs, roadmap   | \"Extract modules from main.rs\"   |\n139: | **Sweep**   | Fix a cluster of related bugs                 | Bug tracker, gap doc         | \"Multi-cursor selection bugs\"    |\n140: \n141: This prevents scope creep and keeps AI contributions coherent across sessions.\n142: \n143: ---\n144: \n145: ### Design Before Code\n146: \n147: For complex features, invest in upfront documentation:\n148: \n149: **1. Reference Documentation**  \n150: Create a \"document of truth\" for cross-cutting concerns. [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) defines\n151: viewport math, coordinate systems, and scrolling behavior—used across split view, selection, and overlay\n152: implementations.\n153: \n154: **2. Feature Specifications**  \n155: Before implementing multi-cursor, we wrote [SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md](docs/archived/SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md):\n156: \n157: - Data structures and invariants\n158: - Keyboard shortcuts table\n159: - Message enums and expected behavior\n160: - Multi-phase implementation plan\n161: \n162: **3. Gap Documents**  \n163: When a feature is 60-90% complete, create a gap\n164: doc. [MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md](docs/archived/MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md) lists:\n165: \n166: - What's implemented vs. missing\n167: - Design decisions for each gap\n168: - Tests and success criteria\n169: \n170: This turns \"vague incompleteness\" into concrete, actionable tasks.\n171: \n172: ---\n173: \n174: ### The Multi-Cursor Migration: A Case Study\n175: \n176: Adding multi-cursor to a single-cursor editor touched nearly every file. Here's how we avoided chaos:\n177: \n178: **1. Wrote invariants upfront:**\n179: \n180: ```rust\n181: // MUST maintain: cursors.len() == selections.len()\n182: // MUST maintain: cursors[i].to_position() == selections[i].head\n183: ```\n184: \n185: **2. Created migration helpers:**\n186: \n187: ```rust\n188: // Old code still works via accessor\n189: impl AppModel {\n190:     pub fn cursor(&self) -> &Cursor { &self.editor.cursors[0] }\n191: }\n192: ```\n193: \n194: **3. Implemented in phases:**\n195: \n196: - Phase 0: Per-cursor primitives (`move_cursor_left_at(idx)`)\n197: - Phase 1: All-cursor wrappers (`move_all_cursors_left()`)\n198: - Phase 2-4: Update handlers, add tests\n199: - Phase 5: Bug sweep for edge cases\n200: \n201: **4. Ran targeted sweeps:**\n202: When bugs emerged, we created a focused tracker and fixed them systematically—not one-off. This leverages AI's strength\n203: at \"apply this pattern everywhere.\"\n204: \n205: ---\n206: \n207: ### Agent Configuration\n208: \n209: Tell agents how to work in your codebase:\n210: \n211: | File        | Purpose                                         |\n212: | ----------- | ----------------------------------------------- |\n213: | `AGENTS.md` | Build commands, architecture, conventions       |\n214: | `CLAUDE.md` | Same (symlink or duplicate for different tools) |\n215: \n216: Key: specify your Makefile/scripts so agents use `make test` instead of inventing\n217: `cargo test --all-features --no-fail-fast`.\n218: \n219: ---\n220: \n221: ### Documentation Structure\n222: \n223: | Path                                                       | Purpose                                             |\n224: | ---------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |\n225: | [docs/ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md)                         | Planned features with design doc links              |\n226: | [docs/CHANGELOG.md](docs/CHANGELOG.md)                     | Completed work (reference when things break)        |\n227: | [docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) | Domain reference (geometry, coordinates, scrolling) |\n228: | `docs/feature/`                                            | Design specs for planned features                   |\n229: | `docs/archived/`                                           | Completed feature specs (kept for reference)        |\n230: \n231: ---\n232: \n233: ### Agent Workflow Patterns\n234: \n235: **Research → Synthesize → Implement:**\n236: \n237: 1. Use **Librarian** to research how VSCode/Zed/Helix solve a problem\n238: 2. Use **Oracle** to review findings and produce a design doc\n239: 3. Implement in phases with tests\n240: \n241: **Review Before Implementation:**\n242: Having Oracle review [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) caught 15+ issues (off-by-one errors,\n243: division-by-zero edge cases) before they became bugs in code.\n244: \n245: ---\n246: \n247: ## Development Timeline\n248: \n249: Token's development followed distinct phases, each with focused objectives:\n250: \n251: | Phase               | Dates          | Focus                                           |\n252: | ------------------- | -------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |\n253: | Foundation          | Sep 26 - Dec 5 | Setup, reference docs, architecture             |\n254: | Research Sprint     | Dec 6          | Performance, keymaps, testing infrastructure    |\n255: | Feature Development | Dec 5-6        | Split view, undo/redo, multi-cursor selection   |\n256: | Codebase Refactor   | Dec 6          | Extract modules from main.rs (3100→20 lines)    |\n257: | Research & Polish   | Dec 7          | Zed research, cursor API fixes, test extraction |\n258: | Maintenance         | Dec 7          | Bugfixes, benchmarks, documentation             |\n259: | Keymapping System   | Dec 15         | Configurable YAML keybindings, 74 default bindings |\n260: | Syntax Highlighting | Dec 15         | Tree-sitter integration, 17 languages supported |\n261: | CSV Viewer/Editor   | Dec 16         | Spreadsheet view with cell editing (Phases 1-2) |\n262: | HiDPI Fixes         | Dec 16         | Display switching, ScaledMetrics system         |\n263: | Workspace Management| Dec 17         | Sidebar file tree, focus system, global shortcuts |\n264: \n265: ---\n266: \n267: ### Notable Threads\n268: \n269: <details>\n270: <summary><strong>🔮 Oracle: UI Reference Deep Review</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-7b92a860-a2f7-4397-985c-73b2fa3e9582\">T-7b92a860</a></summary>\n271: \n272: **Date**: 2025-12-03\n273: \n274: The Oracle performed a comprehensive technical review of EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md and identified **15+ issues**:\n275: \n276: **Critical Bugs Found**:\n277: \n278: - Off-by-one error in viewport calculations: `lastVisibleLine` should be `firstVisibleLine + visibleLines - 1`\n279: - Division-by-zero edge cases in scrollbar thumb calculations\n280: - `preferredColumn` was documented as column index but code used pixel X values\n281: \n282: **Semantic Issues**:\n283: \n284: - Selection struct uses `anchor/head` internally but docs said `start/end`\n285: - Missing coverage for folding, soft-wrap interaction, IME composition, BiDi text\n286: \n287: **Impact**: Created systematic AMP_REPORT.md with prioritized fixes. Prevented 1-3h of debugging per issue.\n288: \n289: **Lesson Learned**: Always have oracle review reference docs before implementation - catches subtle algorithmic bugs\n290: that tests miss.\n291: \n292: </details>\n293: \n294: <details>\n295: <summary><strong>📚 Librarian: Keymap System Design</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-35b11d40-96b0-4177-9c75-4c723dfd8f80\">T-35b11d40</a></summary>\n296: \n297: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n298: \n299: **Research Question**: How do major editors implement configurable keyboard mappings?\n300: \n301: **Projects Studied**:\n302: | Editor | Pattern | Key Insight |\n303: |--------|---------|-------------|\n304: | VSCode | Flat vector + context | User overrides predictable via insertion order |\n305: | Helix | Trie-based | Efficient for prefix matching in modal editors |\n306: | Zed | Flat with depth indexing | Context depth + insertion order for precedence |\n307: | Neovim | Lua-based | Full scripting for complex mappings |\n308: \n309: **Key Finding**: Two dominant patterns - **trie-based** for modal editors (Helix) vs **flat vector with indexing** for\n310: context-rich editors (Zed, VSCode).\n311: \n312: **Outcome**: Created [docs/feature/KEYMAPPING.md](docs/feature/KEYMAPPING.md) design doc with data structures, TOML\n313: config format, and phased implementation plan.\n314: \n315: </details>\n316: \n317: <details>\n318: <summary><strong>🐛 Critical Bug: Cmd+Z Was Typing 'z'!</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-519a8c9d-b94f-45e5-98e0-5bfc34c77cbf\">T-519a8c9d</a></summary>\n319: \n320: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n321: \n322: **The Bug**: Undo/redo keyboard shortcuts were completely broken on macOS. Pressing Cmd+Z inserted the letter 'z' into\n323: the document instead of undoing!\n324: \n325: **Root Cause**: Key handler only checked `control_key()` modifier, not `super_key()` (macOS Command key).\n326: \n327: **The Fix**:\n328: \n329: ```rust\n330: // Before (broken on macOS)\n331: if modifiers.control_key() & & key == \"z\" { ... }\n332: \n333: // After (cross-platform)\n334: if (modifiers.control_key() | | modifiers.super_key()) & & key == \"z\" { ... }\n335: ```\n336: \n337: **Lesson Learned**: Always handle both `control_key()` AND `super_key()` for cross-platform keyboard shortcuts. The\n338: macOS Command key is a completely different modifier than Ctrl.\n339: \n340: </details>\n341: \n342: <details>\n343: <summary><strong>🏗️ Split View: EditorArea Architecture</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-29b1dd08-eee1-44fb-abd5-eb982d6bcd52\">T-29b1dd08</a></summary>\n344: \n345: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n346: \n347: **Problem**: Implement multi-pane editing with tabs, splits, and shared documents.\n348: \n349: **Architectural Decisions**:\n350: \n351: 1. **Shared documents, independent editors**: Documents stored in `HashMap<DocumentId, Document>`, editors in\n352:    `HashMap<EditorId, EditorState>`. Multiple editors can view the same document with independent cursors/viewports.\n353: \n354: 2. **Layout tree structure**: `LayoutNode` is either `Group(GroupId)` or `Split(SplitContainer)`. Recursive structure\n355:    allows arbitrary nesting.\n356: \n357: 3. **Migration helper**: `EditorArea::single_document()` provides backward compatibility:\n358: \n359: ```rust\n360: impl AppModel {\n361:     pub fn document(&self) -> &Document {\n362:         self.editor_area.focused_document()  // Hides complexity\n363:     }\n364: }\n365: ```\n366: \n367: **Lesson Learned**: Migration helpers like `single_document()` are crucial for phased refactoring. The pattern of \"\n368: replace two fields with one complex struct + accessors\" worked smoothly.\n369: \n370: </details>\n371: \n372: <details>\n373: <summary><strong>🖱️ Multi-Cursor: Only Primary Cursor Moved!</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-d4c75d42-c0c1-4746-a609-593bff88db6d\">T-d4c75d42</a></summary>\n374: \n375: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n376: \n377: **The Bug**: Arrow keys and all movement operations only affected the primary cursor. Secondary cursors were frozen!\n378: \n379: **Root Cause**: All movement handlers assumed single cursor - `move_cursor_up()` operated on `cursor_mut()` which only\n380: returned `cursors[0]`.\n381: \n382: **The Fix - Per-Cursor Primitives Pattern**:\n383: \n384: ```rust\n385: // Low-level: operates on one cursor\n386: fn move_cursor_left_at(&mut self, doc: &Document, idx: usize) { ... }\n387: \n388: // High-level: iterates all cursors\n389: fn move_all_cursors_left(&mut self, doc: &Document) {\n390:     for idx in 0..self.cursors.len() {\n391:         self.move_cursor_left_at(doc, idx);\n392:     }\n393: }\n394: ```\n395: \n396: **Critical Invariant**:\n397: \n398: ```rust\n399: // MUST maintain: cursors.len() == selections.len()\n400: // MUST maintain: cursors[i].to_position() == selections[i].head\n401: ```\n402: \n403: **Lesson Learned**: Always question single-item assumptions when adding multi-item support. The \"per-item primitive +\n404: all-items wrapper\" pattern scales well.\n405: \n406: </details>\n407: \n408: <details>\n409: <summary><strong>🔀 Selection Merging: merge_overlapping_selections()</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-e751be48-ab56-4b90-a196-d5df892d955b\">T-e751be48</a></summary>\n410: \n411: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n412: \n413: **The Problem**: After SelectWord with multiple cursors on adjacent words, selections would overlap and cause rendering\n414: bugs.\n415: \n416: **The Solution**: `merge_overlapping_selections()` maintains the parallel array invariant:\n417: \n418: ```rust\n419: pub fn merge_overlapping_selections(&mut self) {\n420:     // 1. Sort by start position\n421:     // 2. Merge consecutive overlapping/touching ranges\n422:     // 3. Update both cursors and selections arrays in lockstep\n423: }\n424: ```\n425: \n426: **Key Insight**: \"Touching\" selections like `[0,5)` and `[5,10)` should merge into `[0,10)` - matches user expectations\n427: for continuous selections.\n428: \n429: </details>\n430: \n431: <details>\n432: <summary><strong>📦 Module Extraction Sprint</strong> (7 threads, Dec 6)</summary>\n433: \n434: **Date**: 2025-12-06\n435: \n436: Systematic extraction from monolithic files:\n437: \n438: | Before      | After                                      | Lines            |\n439: | ----------- | ------------------------------------------ | ---------------- |\n440: | `main.rs`   | `app.rs`, `input.rs`, `view.rs`, `perf.rs` | 3100 → 20        |\n441: | `update.rs` | `update/` module directory (5 submodules)  | 2900 → organized |\n442: \n443: **Threads**: T-ce688bab → T-072af2cb (7 sequential extractions)\n444: \n445: **Result**: 669 tests passing, cleaner architecture, easier navigation.\n446: \n447: **Commits**: [`f602368`](https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token/commit/f602368), [\n448: `71a3b87`](https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token/commit/71a3b87)\n449: \n450: </details>\n451: \n452: <details>\n453: <summary><strong>🔬 Librarian: Zed GPUI Deep Dive</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-c764b2bc-4b0b-4a2a-8c65-c11460405741\">T-c764b2bc</a></summary>\n454: \n455: **Date**: 2025-12-07\n456: \n457: **Research Topic**: How does Zed's GPUI framework handle rendering and state?\n458: \n459: **Key Discoveries**:\n460: \n461: - **Three-phase render cycle**: `request_layout` → `prepaint` → `paint`\n462: - **Entity-based state**: Reference-counted handles with `notify()` for reactivity\n463: - **DispatchTree**: Hierarchical focus management and event routing\n464: - **Taffy layout engine**: Flexbox-style layout calculations\n465: \n466: **Patterns for Token**:\n467: \n468: - Modal/overlay system with hitbox blocking\n469: - Deferred draw for overlays\n470: - Focus trapping with hitbox system\n471: \n472: </details>\n473: \n474: <details>\n475: <summary><strong>⌨️ Keymapping System v0.2.0</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b217e-cd52-76ce-bdf5-f17b8d46105d\">T-019b217e</a></summary>\n476: \n477: **Date**: 2025-12-15\n478: \n479: **Problem**: Hardcoded keybindings in input.rs made customization impossible and led to precedence issues.\n480: \n481: **Solution**: Complete migration to YAML-configurable keymapping system:\n482: \n483: ```yaml\n484: # keymap.yaml - platform-aware, context-sensitive bindings\n485: - key: cmd+s\n486:   command: Save\n487: - key: tab\n488:   command: Indent\n489:   when: [has_selection]\n490: - key: tab\n491:   command: InsertTab\n492:   when: [no_selection]\n493: ```\n494: \n495: **Key Implementation Details**:\n496: \n497: - 74 default bindings embedded at compile time via `include_str!()`\n498: - Platform-aware `cmd` modifier (Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere)\n499: - Context conditions: `has_selection`, `has_multiple_cursors`, `modal_active`, etc.\n500: - Chord support with `KeyAction::{Execute, AwaitMore, NoMatch}`\n501: - `command: Unbound` pattern to disable default bindings\n502: \n503: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b0309, T-019b0323, T-019b2111, T-019b2125, T-019b217e\n504: \n505: </details>\n506: \n507: <details>\n508: <summary><strong>🌈 Syntax Highlighting with Tree-sitter</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b22cc-7eae-750e-92dc-a62e57a18d8f\">T-019b22cc</a></summary>\n509: \n510: **Date**: 2025-12-15\n511: \n512: **Problem**: No syntax highlighting - all code displayed as plain text.\n513: \n514: **Solution**: Async tree-sitter-based syntax highlighting with 17 language support:\n515: \n516: - **Background worker thread** with mpsc channels for non-blocking parsing\n517: - **30ms debounce** prevents parsing on every keystroke\n518: - **Revision tracking** discards stale parse results\n519: - **Incremental parsing** with `tree.edit()` for efficient updates\n520: \n521: **Supported Languages**: Rust, YAML, Markdown, HTML, CSS, JavaScript, TypeScript, JSON, TOML, Python, Go, PHP, C, C++, Java, Bash\n522: \n523: **Architecture**:\n524: \n525: ```\n526: Edit → DebouncedSyntaxParse → ParseReady → Worker Thread → ParseCompleted → Apply Highlights\n527: ```\n528: \n529: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b2280, T-019b22cc, T-019b22f1, T-019b2307, T-019b230f, T-019b238a, T-019b239e\n530: \n531: </details>\n532: \n533: <details>\n534: <summary><strong>📊 CSV Viewer with Cell Editing</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b2783-7db1-73cf-b7de-2373fcbb61f0\">T-019b2783</a></summary>\n535: \n536: **Date**: 2025-12-16\n537: \n538: **Problem**: CSV files displayed as raw text with no structure.\n539: \n540: **Solution**: Spreadsheet-style CSV viewer with cell editing:\n541: \n542: - **Phase 1**: Grid rendering, row/column headers, cell navigation, mouse wheel scrolling\n543: - **Phase 2**: Cell editing with proper CSV escaping (RFC 4180), document synchronization\n544: \n545: **Key Features**:\n546: \n547: - Toggle via Command Palette: \"Toggle CSV View\"\n548: - Delimiter detection from file extension or content (CSV, TSV, PSV)\n549: - Keyboard navigation: Arrow keys, Tab/Shift+Tab, Page Up/Down, Cmd+Home/End\n550: - Edit with Enter or start typing, Escape to cancel\n551: \n552: **Stress Tested**: Handles 10k+ row files with virtual scrolling\n553: \n554: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b22bd, T-019b2734, T-019b274f, T-019b275d, T-019b2783, T-019b2794\n555: \n556: </details>\n557: \n558: <details>\n559: <summary><strong>📁 Workspace Management & File Tree</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b2b7a-8dd7-763a-9ab7-3132ddcf516a\">T-019b2b7a</a></summary>\n560: \n561: **Date**: 2025-12-17\n562: \n563: **Problem**: No file browser - users had to open files via command line or dialogs.\n564: \n565: **Solution**: Full workspace management with sidebar file tree:\n566: \n567: - **File tree sidebar** with expand/collapse folders, file icons\n568: - **Focus management system**: `FocusTarget` enum (Editor, Sidebar, Modal)\n569: - **Mouse interaction**: Double-click to open files, single-click chevron for folders\n570: - **Keyboard navigation**: Arrow keys, Enter to open, Escape to return to editor\n571: - **Theming**: Full sidebar color customization in theme YAML\n572: \n573: **Architecture**:\n574: \n575: ```rust\n576: struct Workspace {\n577:     root: PathBuf,\n578:     file_tree: FileTree,\n579:     sidebar_visible: bool,\n580:     sidebar_width: f32,\n581: }\n582: ```\n583: \n584: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b2b7a, T-019b2bc5, T-019b2be7, T-019b2c40, T-019b2c8a, T-019b2ca6\n585: \n586: </details>\n587: \n588: <details>\n589: <summary><strong>🖥️ HiDPI Display Scaling Fixes</strong> | <a href=\"https://ampcode.com/threads/T-019b2476-55a8-7058-9ba9-9360a9280c1b\">T-019b2476</a></summary>\n590: \n591: **Date**: 2025-12-16\n592: \n593: **Problem**: Tab area too small on Retina displays, debug overlay misaligned - scaling broken on HiDPI.\n594: \n595: **Root Cause Investigation**:\n596: \n597: - Metrics calculated in logical units but used as physical pixels\n598: - Font sizes not scaled by DPI factor\n599: - Inconsistent coordinate systems between rendering and hit testing\n600: \n601: **The Fix - ScaledMetrics**:\n602: \n603: ```rust\n604: pub struct ScaledMetrics {\n605:     pub tab_bar_height: u32,      // Pre-scaled by scale_factor\n606:     pub status_bar_height: u32,\n607:     pub line_height: u32,\n608:     pub char_width: u32,\n609:     // ... all measurements pre-scaled\n610: }\n611: ```\n612: \n613: **Key Insight**: Scale once at metrics creation, use physical pixels everywhere else.\n614: \n615: **Threads in this feature**: T-019b2476, T-019b26ca, T-019b26e5\n616: \n617: </details>\n618: \n619: ---\n620: \n621: <details>\n622: <summary><strong>📋 Full Thread Reference (108 threads)</strong></summary>\n623: \n624: All conversations are public. {
  "content": "1: //! Unified text editing system for the Token editor.\n2: //!\n3: //! This module provides a unified abstraction for text editing across all contexts:\n4: //! - Main document editor (multi-line, multi-cursor)\n5: //! - Command palette input (single-line)\n6: //! - Go-to-line dialog (single-line, numeric only)\n7: //! - Find/Replace inputs (single-line)\n8: //! - CSV cell editor (single-line)\n9: //!\n10: //! # Architecture\n11: //!\n12: //! The core components are:\n13: //!\n14: //! - [`TextBuffer`] / [`TextBufferMut`]: Traits abstracting over buffer implementations\n15: //! - [`StringBuffer`]: Buffer for single-line inputs (backed by `String`)\n16: //! - [`RopeBuffer`]: Buffer for multi-line documents (backed by `ropey::Rope`)\n17: //! - [`EditableState`]: Main state container with cursor, selection, and history\n18: //! - [`EditConstraints`]: Context-specific restrictions\n19: //! - [`EditContext`]: Identifies which editing context a message is for\n20: //! - [`TextEditMsg`]: Unified message type for all editing operations\n21: //!\n22: //! # Example\n23: //!\n24: //! ```ignore\n25: //! use token::editable::{EditableState, StringBuffer, EditConstraints};\n26: //!\n27: //! // Create a single-line input\n28: //! let mut state = EditableState::new(\n29: //!     StringBuffer::from_text(\"hello\"),\n30: //!     EditConstraints::single_line(),\n31: //! );\n32: //!\n33: //! // Move cursor and edit\n34: //! state.move_word_right(false);\n35: //! state.insert_char('!');\n36: //!\n37: //! assert_eq!(state.text(), \"hello!\");\n38: //! ```\n39: \n40: mod buffer;\n41: mod constraints;\n42: mod context;\n43: mod cursor;\n44: mod history;\n45: mod messages;\n46: mod selection;\n47: mod state;\n48: \n49: // Re-export main types\n50: pub use buffer::{RopeBuffer, StringBuffer, TextBuffer, TextBufferMut};\n51: pub use constraints::{CharFilter, EditConstraints};\n52: pub use context::EditContext;\n53: pub use cursor::{Cursor, Position};\n54: pub use history::{EditHistory, EditOperation};\n55: pub use messages::{MoveTarget, TextEditMsg};\n56: pub use selection::Selection;\n57: pub use state::EditableState;",
  "absolutePath": "~/code/rust-editor/src/editable/mod.rs"
}
